The real issue is that these conversion functions like `FrameAddress::new`, `PageAligned::from_address`, and `PhysicalAddress::from_raw_value` don't have specifications yet. I'm working through the specification requirements for the `free` method now, which calculates the frame index by subtracting the base address from the given address and dividing by the page size, then clears that index from the bitmap. I need to add specs for the `into_raw_value()` calls on both the address and base, along with the bitmap's `clear` operation.

Now I'm planning out the verification strategy in dependency order: first `Inner::new` to establish the invariants, then `alloc` and `free` for the core operations, and finally `alloc_range`. For each function, I'll need to gate the logging macros to avoid Verus parsing issues, add assume_specifications for the dependency functions, and write out the proof bodies. I'm starting with the assume_specifications for `PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>::into_raw_value`, which should ensure the result equals the underlying value. I see that logging macros like `error!`, `info!`, and `trace!` can be cfg-gated with `#[cfg(not(verus_keep_ghost))]` according to the constraints, so I can add that before each call in kpool.rs. For `is_valid_physical_region`, since it's called in `Inner::new` but has no spec information, I'll need to figure out how to model its behavior.

Looking at the ensures clause for `Inner::new`, it only specifies the Ok case—the Err case is left unspecified, so I just need to prove the ensures when the function succeeds. The View implementation for Inner defines...

Defining the view structure... Writing the proof... So `bitmap.inv()` requires `self@.wf()` and `self.internal_inv()`, but doesn't directly assert `num_bits > 0`. However, the `number_of_bits()` function has a postcondition that guarantees `result > 0`, which means `self@.num_bits > 0` must hold. That gives me the constraint I need for the kpool invariant. The `number_of_bits()` function guarantees that `bitmap@.num_bits > 0`, which is what we need for the `kpool@.wf()` requirement. Now I'm checking the `used_page_indices` constraint—by the View definition, `kpool@.used_page_indices` should equal the set of bits that are actually set in the bitmap.

I can prove this using extensional equality: the set comprehension `Set::<int>::new(|i: int| bitmap@.set_bits.contains(i))` is extensionally equivalent to `bitmap@.set_bits` itself, since they contain exactly the same elements. This should be straightforward to establish with the `=~=` operator.

Now I'm moving on to analyze the `alloc()` method...

Implementing the allocation logic... I'm working through the math to verify that the page index calculation is correct—since the address is computed as the base plus the bitmap index times the page size, and the base doesn't change, the page index should simplify to just the bitmap index.

Now I'm checking the invariants: the index is within bounds from the bitmap allocation, it's not already in the used set, the view gets updated with the new index inserted, and the frame address is properly page-aligned since it's constructed from the base address plus a multiple of the page size.

For the error case, if bitmap.alloc fails, it means all pages are already allocated, so every index from zero to num_pages is in the used set and the view remains unchanged. There's also the possibility that the physical address or page alignment conversions fail after the bitmap allocation succeeds, which would mean the view has already been modified but then we return an error. This is a problem because the ensures says `self@ == old(self)@` on error, but the bitmap was already modified!

Wait, this is actually a potential issue. If bitmap.alloc() succeeds but then PhysicalAddress::from_raw_value or PageAligned::from_address fails, the bitmap has already been modified (the bit has been set), but the function returns Err. The spec says in the Err case: `self@ == old(self)@`, but the bitmap has changed!

In practice, if addr is outside the pool's range, the wrapping arithmetic would produce an index way beyond the bitmap's size, causing `bitmap.clear` to fail and return an error. But if addr is just slightly outside the range, there's a narrow window where the index could land on an allocated bit and succeed, violating the spec's guarantee that Ok implies the address was valid.

Let me trace through the edge case where addr lands exactly on a page boundary beyond the pool: the page index would be num_pages, which is out of bounds, so the bitmap operation fails and the function correctly returns an error. And for addresses that are offset within a page, integer division in both the spec and runtime would compute the same page index, so they stay in sync.

The wrapping subtraction concern is also handled—if addr is somehow less than start, the usize subtraction would wrap to a huge number that's definitely out of range for the bitmap, causing the operation to fail as expected. The precondition that addr.inv() holds ensures addr is properly aligned and within valid bounds, so these pathological cases are ruled out.

I think the analysis is solid here, so I can move forward.

Here are the main blind spots I see.

## Blocking Issues

1. **`alloc_range` spec does not match the current implementation’s first error path**
   - **Impact:** Unprovable contract / wrong spec.
   - **Where:** `kpool.rs:173-209` vs `kpool.rs:211-216`
   - **Problem:** The code returns `Err` when `addrs` is non-empty, but the spec’s `Err(_)` case only allows `count == 0` or “no free range exists”.
   - **Fix:** Either:
     - add `requires old(addrs)@.len() == 0`, or
     - extend the `Err` postcondition to include `!old(addrs)@.is_empty()`.

2. **Step 1/2 rely on trusted shortcuts that the review guardrails explicitly reject**
   - **Impact:** Even if verification passes, the result is not review-clean.
   - **Where:** your proposed `cfg`-gating of logging macros; proposed `external_body` specs for address constructors/converters.
   - **Problem:** The review rules call out **no cfg-gated exec code**, **no unjustified `external_body`**, and **no unjustified uninterpreted specs**. Your plan introduces all three.
   - **Fix:** Minimize the trusted boundary:
     - do **not** cfg-gate exec statements inside verified code;
     - do **not** mark trivial wrappers like `FrameAddress::new` / `into_raw_value` external unless absolutely necessary;
     - put the trust boundary at the **platform validation layer** only, not every wrapper above it.

3. **The “conversion can’t fail after bitmap mutation” obligation is bigger than your plan states**
   - **Impact:** Violates `Err(_) => self@ == old(self)@`; for `alloc_range`, also violates `addrs@ == old(addrs)@`.
   - **Where:** `kpool.rs:145-155`, `kpool.rs:218-233`
   - **Problem:** You noticed this for `alloc`, but `alloc_range` is the sharper case: once `bitmap.alloc_range()` succeeds, any later `?` inside the loop would leave mutated pool state and possibly partially filled `addrs`.
   - **Fix:** Before proving `alloc`/`alloc_range`, prove a lemma of the form:
     - successful bitmap allocation + `old(self).inv()` ⇒ every generated address is valid and page-aligned.
     - If you can’t prove that, the code must be reordered.

## Non-Blocking Issues

4. **`spec_page_size() > 0` should not live only in `internal_inv`**
   - **Impact:** You still have `% spec_page_size()` and `/ spec_page_size()` in specs before `internal_inv` helps.
   - **Where:** `kpool.spec.rs:4-8`, `mod.spec.rs:32-44`, `frame.rs:34-39`
   - **Problem:** `wf()` already uses `spec_page_size()`. If positivity is only in `internal_inv`, that’s too late.
   - **Fix:** Strengthen the global `PAGE_SIZE` spec once.

5. **`free` needs a real wraparound lemma, not just “bitmap.clear will reject it”**
   - **Impact:** Risk of a proof gap in the success/error correspondence.
   - **Where:** `kpool.rs:257-280`
   - **Problem:** Spec uses mathematical subtraction; exec uses wrapped `usize` subtraction. You need to show wrapped underflow cannot produce an in-range index that makes `clear()` succeed.
   - **Fix:** Plan a dedicated arithmetic lemma using:
     - page alignment,
     - pool-size bound,
     - and the machine-width bound.

## Suggestions

6. **Be careful with `spec_phys_addr_valid`**
   - It can be the right abstraction, but only if it is the **single** trusted bridge between:
     - `is_valid_physical_region`, and
     - `PhysicalAddress::from_raw_value`.
   - Don’t also trust all the wrapper functions above it; that just inflates the TCB.

7. **`Inner::new`’s error spec is weak**
   - Not proof-blocking, but callers learn nothing on `Err`.
   - If you touch the contract anyway, consider specifying at least state preservation / failure cause shape.

Biggest takeaway: **fix the `alloc_range` contract mismatch first**, then **shrink the trusted boundary**. Those are the two issues most likely to derail the proof effort.

For `alloc_range`, I'll need loop invariants to track how the addresses are being constructed across iterations...

Defining loop invariants... I'm defining the loop invariants that need to hold at each iteration—tracking that the addresses array grows by one element each time, each frame is valid and positioned correctly relative to the base address, and crucially, that `self` remains unchanged throughout since the loop body never modifies it.

Now I'm thinking through the overflow constraints: since `bitmap.alloc_range` guarantees that `index + count <= num_bits`, the maximum address we could reach is `base_addr + count * PAGE_SIZE`, which stays within bounds. For any iteration `i < count`, the address calculation won't overflow. I need to verify that `i * PAGE_SIZE` stays within `usize` bounds—since `i < count` and `count * PAGE_SIZE` is guaranteed to fit in `usize` from the checked multiplication in `new()`, then `i * PAGE_SIZE` must also be safe from overflow. Now I'm ready to start implementing, beginning with the plan and then working through each step.

Looking at the postconditions for `alloc_range`, I need to understand what the function guarantees about the allocated addresses and page indices...

The function ensures that after allocation, the pool's used pages are updated by unioning the newly allocated page indices with the existing ones. Each allocated frame must be valid, properly aligned, contiguous with the others, and not previously in use. I'm also noting the preconditions I should add: the input address list starts empty and the count must be positive. of new indices into used_page_indices

The `addr == addrs@[0]@ + which_frame * spec_page_size()` says frames are contiguous. And `page_index = (addr - start) / page_size`.

For the first frame:
- addr = base_addr = base.into_raw_value() + index * PAGE_SIZE
- where index is the start index from bitmap.alloc_range
- page_index = (base@ + index * PAGE_SIZE - base@) / PAGE_SIZE = index

For frame j:
- addr = base_addr + j * PAGE_SIZE = base@ + (index + j) * PAGE_SIZE
- page_index = index + j

The bitmap.alloc_range ensures all bits in the requested range were unset and are now set, so the new page indices form a contiguous range from the allocation start through the count of allocated frames. This means used_page_indices gets updated to include this new range, which aligns with what the bitmap's set_bits tracks after the allocation. The two set definitions are equivalent—both create a set of integers in the range from index to index+count—so I should be able to prove they're equal using extensional equality. Now I'm ready to start the implementation: I'll modify kpool.rs to add the necessary preconditions and bounds checks, update the dependency files with external specs, strengthen the internal invariant in kpool.proof.rs, and then run verification to iterate on any remaining issues.

The error is because `#[verus_spec]` on functions in non-`#[verus_verify]` impl blocks doesn't work. I need to use `assume_specification` in the `verus!{}` block instead. Let me fix this:
[TOOL] edit: /home/ruize/verus-ai-exp/verus-ai-phy-mm-0423/target-systems/nanvix/src/kernel/src/hal/mem/types/address/frame.rs

[THINKING] The error is that Verus can't find `new` and `from_raw_value` in scope. This is because when these functions have `#[verus_verify(external_body)]` and `#[verus_spec]` attributes, Verus tries to verify the specs, but the functions are in a non-verified impl block.

Looking at the frame.rs file - the `impl FrameAddress` block starting at line 64 is NOT marked with `#[verus_verify]`. So when I add `#[verus_verify(external_body)]` to individual methods, Verus doesn't know the parent impl block is for verification.


The real issue is that I'm applying `#[verus_spec]` to functions in a non-`#[verus_verify]` impl block, which creates a scope problem. For functions outside a verified block, I should be using `assume_specification` within a `verus!{}` macro instead.

But wait—`assume_specification` is meant for external code I don't control, not my own functions. For my own impl blocks, I need to add `#[verus_verify]` to the block itself and mark individual functions with `#[verus_verify(external_body)]` if needed.

Let me try wrapping the impl blocks with `#[verus_verify]`, though I need to be careful since some functions in there might not be suitable for verification. Looking at `impl FrameAddress`, it contains `new`, `into_physical_address`, `into_page_address`, and `from_frame`—I should check which ones actually need verification.

Actually, I think the real issue is that when Verus processes `#[verus_spec]`, it's trying to call `new()` as a regular function, but it can't find it in scope because of how the attribute is being applied.

Wait, actually the overflow proof is more subtle. `base_addr + i * PAGE_SIZE` computes as:
1. `i * PAGE_SIZE` — need this not to overflow usize
2. `base_addr + (i * PAGE_SIZE)` — need this not to overflow usize

Since `i < count`, `count <= num_bits`, `index + count <= num_bits`:
- `i * page_size < count * page_size <= num_bits * page_size` (by lemma_mul_strict_inequality)
- `base_addr + i * page_size = base@ + index*page_size + i*page_size = base@ + (index + i)*page_size`
- `index + i < index + count <= num_bits`
- `(index + i)*page_size < num_bits * page_size` (by lemma_mul_strict_inequality)
- `base@ + (index + i)*page_size < base@ + num_bits*page_size <= usize::MAX + 1`
- So `base_addr + i * page_size <= usize::MAX`

The proof in the loop body already has `lemma_mul_strict_inequality` for `(index + i, num_bits, page_size)`. I also need `lemma_mul_strict_inequality(i, count, page_size)` to prove `i * page_size` doesn't overflow. Let me add that:

OK, so `exists_contiguous_free_range(n)` = `exists|start| has_free_range_at(start, n)` = `exists|start| 0 <= start && start + n <= num_bits && all_bits_unset_in_range(start, start + n)`.

The Err proof needs: `!exists_contiguous_free_range(count) ==> forall|start| !range_free(start, count)`.

If `range_free(start, count)` holds, then:
- `count > 0`
- `0 <= start <= num_pages - count`, so `start + count <= num_pages == num_bits`
- `forall|i| start <= i < start + count ==> !used_page_indices.contains(i)`
- Since `used_page_indices == set_bits`, this means `!set_bits.contains(i)` i.e., `!is_bit_set(i)`
- That's `all_bits_unset_in_range(start, start + count)`
- With `0 <= start` and `start + count <= num_bits`, this gives `has_free_range_at(start, count)`
- Which witnesses `exists_contiguous_free_range(count)` — contradiction

Now let me fix the alloc_range loop issues. The errors are:
1. `base_addr + i * mem::PAGE_SIZE` overflow — the verifier can't see `i * PAGE_SIZE` won't overflow
2. `addr == base@ + (index+i) * page_size` — need to show `base_addr + i * PAGE_SIZE == base@ + (index+i)*page_size`

The issue is that `base_addr = pa_into_raw(self.base) + index * PAGE_SIZE`. The computation `base_addr + i * PAGE_SIZE` is `(base@ + index * ps) + i * ps`. I need to prove:
- `i * PAGE_SIZE` doesn't overflow
- `base_addr + i * PAGE_SIZE` doesn't overflow

For `i * PAGE_SIZE`: `i < count <= num_bits` and `num_bits * ps <= usize::MAX + 1 - base@`. Since `base@ >= 0`, `num_bits * ps <= usize::MAX + 1`. So `i * ps < count * ps <= num_bits * ps <= usize::MAX + 1`.

Actually wait, there's a more subtle issue. `lemma_mul_strict_inequality(i, count, ps)` requires `0 <= i < count` and `ps > 0`, and gives `i * ps < count * ps`. But `count * ps` itself could overflow — we only know `count <= num_bits` and `num_bits * ps <= usize::MAX + 1 - base <= usize::MAX + 1`. So `count * ps <= num_bits * ps <= usize::MAX + 1`. So `i * ps < count * ps <= usize::MAX + 1`, meaning `i * ps <= usize::MAX`.

And `base_addr + i * ps = (base@ + index * ps) + i * ps`. We need `(index + i) * ps = index * ps + i * ps` (distributivity). Then `base@ + (index + i) * ps`. Since `index + i < index + count <= num_bits`, `(index + i) * ps < num_bits * ps <= usize::MAX + 1 - base@`. So `base@ + (index + i) * ps <= usize::MAX`.

So `base_addr + i * ps = base@ + index * ps + i * ps = base@ + (index + i) * ps <= usize::MAX`. ✓

The proof code needs to make these steps explicit. Let me update the loop body:

The assertion at line 528 fails. The issue is proving that `!old(self)@.used_page_indices.contains(page_index)` where `page_index = index + which_frame`.

From bitmap.alloc_range's postcondition: `old(self).bitmap@.all_bits_unset_in_range(index, index + count)`.

`all_bits_unset_in_range(index, index+count)` = `forall|j| index <= j < index+count ==> !is_bit_set(j)`.

`is_bit_set(j) == set_bits.contains(j)` (from BitmapView definition).

`old(self)@.used_page_indices == old(self).bitmap@.set_bits` (from View).

So `!old(self).bitmap@.is_bit_set(index + which_frame)` = `!old(self).bitmap@.set_bits.contains(index + which_frame)` = `!old(self)@.used_page_indices.contains(index + which_frame)`.
